### 593. Job 14:2

### 593.Job 14'2(2_41) trans

Jonathan EdwardsTo a private meeting of young people after Billy Sheldons

death Feb. 1740, 41.

____________________________________________________________

Job 14. 2. He Cometh forth like a flower & is Cut

afterwards preachd the doctrinal Part with the new

application at the End on occasion of the death of my daugh

down ter Jerusha. Feb. 21. 1747, 8.

In these words I would observe 1. what man with Re-

spect to his present Life is Compared to. viz a flower

which [^003-note1]

[-] most commonly is put forth in the spring in the

Pleasantest part of the year and then appears fair

& flourishing & 2. we may observe two

things wherein he is Especially Resembles this that

a flower viz in his beginning & End 1. In

his beginning or his coming forth he is Like

a flower a flower in the spring of the year

soon comes forth it is not Long a putting out

in a day or two after it first begins to put forth

it appears in a full blossom so man Comes

forth quickly he puts forth soon after he comes

into the Θ he Grows up he Comes on to the

stage of action & begins to make a fair &

promising appearance is a Companion amongst

young People is usefull in the family to which

he belongs & is [-] taken notice of by others

Grows in stature & understanding & action

2. He is Like a flower in his End. he is[^003-note2]

cut down Lif Like the flower is soo that soon

Comes forth is soon Gone as it Comes forth in

a day or two so in a day or two it is Gone

presently after the Leaves of the flower are full

spread out it falls the wind blows it away

off from the trees & the flower that Grows

in the field in the midst of its flourishing in

its most blooming state is oftentimes cut

down at once with one stroke of the scythe

____________________________________________________________

In speaking to these words I would 1 shew

how they are applicable to mankind in General

& 3. Especially to those that die in youth

& then make some application Improvement

of spikenard very precious and she brake the Box

deut 32. 35. Their foot shall slide in due

time. The whole verse is -------------

____________

In this verse is Represented the vengeance of G. on

the wicked Isr unbelieving Israelites that were Gods

visible People and Lived under means of Grace &

yet Continued as is express had seen & heard of

such wonderfull works of G. towards that People &

yet Remained as is expressed v. 28. void of

Counsel having no understanding in them & that

under all the Cultivations of Heaven brought forth bitter

& poisonous fruit as is expressed in the two verses

next preceding the text . in the v

________________________________________

These Clause of the verse that I have Chosen for

my text their foot shall slide in due[-] time

seems to Imply the following things to Relating

to that Punishment & destruction that they are

Exposed to. . 1. That they are alwaies exposed

to destruction as one that walking in the stands

in slip or walks in slippery Places is alwaies

exposed to fall. This is Implied in the

move way of their destructions coming upon them

being Represented by their foots sliding. tis

The same is expressed in Ps. 73. 18. surely thou[^008-note1]

didst set them in slippery Places. thou castest them

down to destruction .

________________________________________________________

2. it Implies that they are alwaies exposed to sudden

m unexpected destruction as he that walks in

slippery Places is every moment to fall & when he

falls it comes suddenly & unexpect he can never fore-

see one moment whether he shall stand or fall the

next & when he does fall he falls at once

when he could not foresee it as th which is

also expressed in that 73. Ps. 18. 19. v. surely --

----------- How are they brought down to destruction in

a moment.

________________________________________________________

3. It another thing Implied is that they are tend to fall

of the liable to fall of thems. without being

thrown down by the hand of another.

______________

already & dont fall th now is only that Gods appointed

time is not come . for tis said that when

that due time or appointed com time comes they

shall slid there foot shall slide. then they

shall be Lost G. wont hold em up in those slippery

places any Longer & then but will Let em Go

& there they will fall of thems. the as a man

that stands in such slippery Places that he Cant stand alone

then in a moment he shall fall into destruction

doc. There is nothing that Keeps wicked men[^008-note2]

at Each moment out of Hell but the meer

pleasure of G.

### 620. Is. 27:13

### 620.Is. 27'13(6_41) trans

Jonathan EdwardsJune 1741.

________________________________________________________________________

Isai. 27. 13. And it shall come to pass in that day

that the great Trumpet shall be blown and they

shall come which were Ready to Perish in the Land

of Assyria, and the outcasts in the Land of Egypt

and shall worship the L. in the Holy mount

at Jerusalem .

__________________________

In this & the two f Three Preceding Chapters are

foretold Certain Great things that shall be should be

accomplished at a Certain future day & theref we

have repeatedly in these Chapters this Expression

in that day . shall this & that come to pass.

& we Learn what day it is by the things that are

spoken to be accomplished in that day. Chap. 24.

21 22. 23 -----------------------------------------------

-------------------------------

25. Chap. 9v -----------------------------

26. Chap. 1.2.v. ------------------------------------

27. Chap. 1.2.3.v. ----------------------------

2 -------------------------------------------

12. & 13. verses . --------------------------------

-------------------------------------

The things spoken shew the day to be the Glorious

Gospel day . the day when G. shall set up the

K. of his son on the Earth

in the Text we may observe .[L. 1v.]

1. The Redemption of Gods People foretold

of which two things are to Observed there are

two parts which Consists in two things

In p which may be observed two things .

1 what the

Concerning which two things are here Represented

1. The miserable state they are saved from which

is a state of Captivity & Banishment here represented

by the state that the poor captives & outcasts &
